He works at that auto body shop down on Main Street, the one with the giant inflatable waving tube guy out front? I always wonder how much electricity those things use, just flapping around all day... but yeah, I don't really feel down, just heavy.\n\nDoctor: Do you find pleasure in activities you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess... I mean, things I usually love just feel really flat now. Like, I'm only pretending to care about them. Usually I love cooking for myself, or playing my guitar, or even just going over to my family's place. Or hiking! I used to hike up at Miller's Ridge all the time. The trails up there are so nice in the fall when the leaves change. My brother went up there last weekend and said he saw a black bear, just right off the path. Can you imagine? He said it was just eating berries or something, didn't even care he was there. But he had his big camera with him—he got really into photography after he broke his leg and couldn't play softball anymore. He bought this huge lens that costs more than my car. My car's been making this weird rattling noise lately, actually, right when I hit 40 miles an hour. I should probably get that looked at, but... yeah, I just don't have the energy to care about my guitar or cooking or any of it right now. It's just all... flat.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, it's been really messed up lately. With the changing shifts at work, I got to where I’d sleep only a few hours and still feel totally wired. My manager keeps messing with the schedule. One week I'm opening, the next I'm closing. She uses this weird app to send out the shifts and it never updates right on my phone. My phone is so old though, the screen is cracked right across the front camera so all my selfies look like I'm underwater. I dropped it in the parking lot at the grocery store. The pavement there is terrible, full of potholes. But yeah, now even when I’m completely exhausted, I wake up super early and my mind is just already going, like a million miles an hour.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or on edge?\n\nPatient: Anxious? Um... not really. I don't feel like, nervous or anything. Just... like I said, my mind is just going so fast. It's not a worried fast, just... fast. Like when you're on the highway and everyone is zooming by. The highway by my apartment is getting totally repaved right now. They start construction at like, 5 AM, with those huge floodlights and all the beeping trucks backing up. Beep, beep, beep. It drives my cat absolutely crazy. She just hides under the sofa all morning. I bought her this special calming collar, it smells like lavender, but she managed to hook her claw in it and rip it right off. She's a smart cat, part Siamese, I think. She has those bright blue eyes. But no, I wouldn't say I feel anxious or on edge, really. Just... restless, maybe.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: I don't know, not really. Like I was saying before, the stuff I usually love just... isn't doing it for me. Cooking, playing my guitar, even hiking. It's all just really flat. I feel like I'm just going through the motions, you know? Pretending to care. Like when you're at a party and you have to smile at someone's baby even though you don't really like babies. My cousin just had a baby, actually. A little girl. They named her Harper. It's a cute name, but they painted her nursery this really aggressive shade of yellow. Like a school bus. I told them it might be too bright for a baby to sleep in, but they said they wanted something cheerful. Cheerful is fine, but you need to sleep! They bought all this expensive furniture for the room too, a crib that converts into a toddler bed and then a full-size bed. It seems like a smart idea, but who knows if she'll even want that bed when she's a teenager. Teenagers are so picky. I remember when I was a teenager, I insisted on painting my room black. My mom was furious. But yeah... I just can't seem to find the joy in my usual things right now.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um, well, the really low part... the empty feeling... that's been going on for about four months now. It started way after that initial rush of energy I had when the work shifts first got all crazy. Four months is a long time, actually. It's like a whole season. Summer just kind of came and went and I barely noticed. I usually love summer, you know? The smell of sunscreen and chlorine. My friend Sarah has a pool, an above-ground one, but it's still nice. We used to sit out there on these cheap plastic lounge chairs that always left weird lines on the backs of your legs. We'd drink those hard seltzers, the black cherry ones. I don't really like the lime ones, they taste like cleaning supplies. Like Pine-Sol or something. My mom used to clean the whole house with Pine-Sol every Saturday morning. You'd wake up and your eyes would practically water from the smell of it. She was obsessed with keeping the floors shiny. But yeah, it's been about four months of just feeling really down and empty.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're empty inside, or is it more that you just can't find pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um, I think it's... it's kind of both, really. Like I said before, the stuff I usually love doing—cooking for myself, playing my guitar, going hiking, or even just going over to my family's place—it all just feels so flat right now. Like I'm just pretending to care about it all. Which is weird because my family usually has these big Sunday dinners. My aunt always makes her famous lasagna. She uses three different kinds of cheese, and she swears the secret is a tiny pinch of nutmeg in the ricotta. I tried making it once, but I accidentally used cinnamon instead of nutmeg. It was awful. My brother actually tried to eat it to be nice, but I could tell he was just choking it down. He's always been a good sport like that. We used to play basketball in the driveway for hours when we were kids until the streetlights came on. The streetlights in my neighborhood now are those new LED ones, and they're so bright they practically shine right through my bedroom window. I had to buy blackout curtains, but they're this heavy velvet material that collects so much dust. I feel like I'm constantly sneezing. But yeah, everything just feels empty and flat, like I'm just going through the motions with all the things I used to enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're sad most of the time, even if it's not always at the front of your mind?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I guess so. I mean, I'm not walking around crying all day, but it's like this heavy blanket is just sitting on me all the time. Even when I'm at work, scanning groceries. The conveyor belt just keeps moving, beep, beep, beep. We had this one lady come in last week who bought literally fifty cans of cat food. The little fancy ones with the pull tabs. She said she was feeding a colony of feral cats behind the old strip mall. I think that's nice of her, but I can't imagine how much that costs. Groceries are so expensive right now. A carton of eggs is ridiculous. I used to make omelets all the time, but now I just eat cereal. Frosted Flakes, usually. Tony the Tiger. They changed the box art recently, did you notice? He looks more... 3D now. I liked the old cartoon version better. But yeah, the sadness is just always kind of there, in the background.\n\nDoctor: Are you still interested in doing things, even if you don't feel like you enjoy them as much?\n\nPatient: Um, not really, no. Like I was saying before, the stuff I usually love doing... cooking for myself, playing my guitar, going hiking, or going over to my family's place... it all just feels so flat right now. I'm not really interested in any of it. It's like I'm just pretending to care. Which is so weird for me, especially the guitar. I have this beautiful acoustic guitar, a Taylor. I saved up for like two years to buy it. I used to keep my tips from the grocery store in this big glass jar on my dresser. It used to be a pickle jar, actually. Vlasic kosher dills. I love pickles. But you have to wash the jar out like five times with hot water and baking soda or else everything you put in it smells like garlic and vinegar. I actually tried to make my own pickles once. I bought all these pickling cucumbers from the farmer's market, and all the spices, the dill seed, the mustard seed. You have to sterilize the jars in boiling water, which made my whole apartment feel like a sauna. It was during that really bad heatwave last summer, too. The AC in my building is terrible. The maintenance guy, Frank, he's always saying he's going to fix it, but he just comes in, bangs on the vent with a wrench, and leaves. He's a nice guy though, he always gives my cat a little treat when he comes over. But yeah... I don't even bother taking the guitar out of the case anymore. I just don't have the interest.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I think so. Like I said before, it's just this heavy blanket feeling. I'm not crying all the time, but the sadness is just... sitting there. Even when I'm just watching TV or something. I was watching this documentary last night about penguins. Emperor penguins, down in Antarctica. It's crazy how they huddle together to stay warm in those blizzards, taking turns being on the outside of the circle. I hate being cold. My apartment gets so drafty in the winter because the windows are old and they don't seal right. I bought that plastic shrink wrap stuff you put over the windows with a hairdryer, but it always peels off at the corners. My cat, Barnaby, loves to chew on the plastic when it comes loose. He's a tuxedo cat, mostly black with little white paws. He knocked over a glass of water on my nightstand yesterday right onto my phone charger. I had to put it in rice. Do you know if that rice trick actually works? I read online that it's a myth and you're just supposed to leave it in front of a fan. But anyway, yeah, the sadness is just pretty constant, even if it's just hanging out in the background.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I guess I have, yeah. My appetite has been totally all over the place. Like, lately I just forget to eat, or when I do, everything just tastes like cardboard. I noticed my work pants have been fitting a lot looser, which is annoying because we have a strict uniform. Khakis and this awful polo shirt. The polo is this really bright, obnoxious red. It clashes with everything. And the fabric is so scratchy, it's like wearing a burlap sack. They make us tuck it in, too, which just looks terrible on everybody. My manager, Brenda, she's a stickler for the uniform. She actually sent a kid home last week because he was wearing black sneakers instead of brown ones. Can you believe that? Black sneakers! Like anyone is looking at the cashier's feet. He's a good kid, too, saving up for college. He wants to study marine biology. I always thought that sounded cool, swimming with dolphins and stuff. But I'm terrified of sharks. I saw Jaws when I was way too young, and it messed me up. I can't even go in the ocean past my knees. But anyway, yeah, I'm definitely losing weight because I'm just not eating much.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Um, not really. I think I mentioned it a minute ago, but yeah, no. Even the stuff I usually love... cooking for myself, playing my guitar, going hiking, or going over to my family's place... it all just feels so flat right now. Like I'm just going through the motions, pretending to care. Which is hard with my family because my mom is always trying to get me to eat. She makes this huge Sunday dinner every week. Roast beef, mashed potatoes, the works. Her gravy is amazing, she uses the drippings and a little bit of red wine. I tried to make it once, but I burnt the flour and the whole house smelled like smoke for two days. I had to open all the windows and it was freezing outside. My neighbor, Mrs. Higgins, came over to check on me because she thought the place was on fire. She's so sweet, she always brings over these little tin boxes of cookies at Christmas. Those Danish butter cookies, you know the ones? But anyway, yeah... I don't really look forward to anything right now. It's just all flat.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a depressed mood most of the time?\n\nPatient: Didn't we kind of just talk about that? Or, wait, you asked if I was sad before. I mean... yeah, I guess it's the same thing, right? Like I was saying with that heavy blanket feeling, it's just dragging me down all day. \n\nIt's funny, the word depressed always makes me think of those old commercials on TV with the little sad bouncing blob or the wind-up toys. I always hated wind-up toys. My grandmother used to collect them and keep them on this glass shelf in her living room. She had this one that was a little monkey clashing cymbals together, and it was honestly terrifying. Its eyes were painted on all wide and staring. She had this whole collection of weird stuff, actually. Like those porcelain dolls that looked like they were watching you sleep. I swear one of them moved its head once when I was staying over, but my mom said it was just the headlights from a car outside making shadows on the wall. We lived right near a really busy intersection back then, so there were always cars going by at all hours. Sometimes you'd hear people yelling out their windows at each other in the middle of the night. \n\nBut yeah... depressed, sad, whatever you want to call it. It's just pretty much there all the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of death or suicide recently?\n\nPatient: I mean... yeah, I've had some scary thoughts lately. Like, sometimes I just think maybe everyone would be better off if I just went to sleep and didn't wake up. And a couple of times, walking home from work, I actually caught myself thinking about just stepping right off the curb into traffic. The traffic on my street is always so bad, especially around rush hour. People drive like absolute maniacs, completely ignoring the speed limit. The city put up one of those digital signs that flashes your speed, but I swear people just use it as a high score board. It's one of those signs with the super bright yellow LED lights. The lights are so blinding, especially when it's raining and it reflects off the wet pavement. I hate walking in the rain. My umbrella always flips inside out because the wind funnels right down my street between the apartment buildings. I had this really cute umbrella with little ducks on it that I got at a thrift store, but the wind absolutely destroyed it last month. I was so mad. The thrift store where I got it is run by this older couple, and they have the most amazing collection of vintage records in the back. But yeah... the thoughts about not waking up or stepping into traffic, they definitely scare me.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past month?\n\nPatient: Um, I haven't really weighed myself, but my appetite has just been completely all over the place. Lately, I mostly just forget to eat entirely, or when I do, the food just tastes like cardboard. But I did notice my work pants have been fitting a lot looser recently. I actually had to go buy a new belt for work because my old one didn't have enough holes in it anymore. I got this really nice leather one from that same thrift store I was talking about. It has this big brass buckle that looks like a horseshoe. I don't even ride horses, but I thought it looked cool. My cousin Sarah used to ride horses when we were kids. She had this beautiful chestnut mare named Cinnamon. Cinnamon was so stubborn, she would always try to bite you if you tried to tighten the saddle too much. Sarah got thrown off once and broke her collarbone. She had to wear this really uncomfortable brace for weeks and couldn't even wash her own hair. Her mom had to help her in the sink, like when you go to the salon. I love going to the salon, the scalp massage they do when they wash your hair is the best part. But yeah, my pants are definitely looser.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things that still bring you a little bit of joy?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I mean, not really. Things I usually love just feel... flat now. Like, I used to love cooking for myself, playing my guitar, going hiking, or even just going over to my family's place. Now it's like I'm only pretending to care about any of it. \n\nMy brother got a new dog recently, a golden retriever puppy, and usually I'd be all over that. I love dogs. When we were growing up, we had this mutt named Buster who would follow us everywhere. He was part terrier, part who-knows-what, and he had this one ear that always stood straight up while the other one flopped over. He used to chase the mail carrier every single day, no matter how many times my dad yelled at him. Our mail carrier back then was this guy named Gary who always wore those really short blue shorts, even in the dead of winter. I never understood how his legs didn't freeze. My legs get cold so easily. I have to wear two pairs of socks to work because standing behind the register all day on that hard tile floor just freezes me right through my shoes. I bought those thick wool socks they sell for camping, but they make my work shoes feel too tight, which gave me a blister on my heel last week. Blisters are the worst, especially when they pop and get all raw. \n\nBut yeah... even seeing the new puppy, I just kind of sat there. I felt like I was supposed to be smiling, but I was just faking it. None of it really brings me joy right now.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Like I'm just going through the motions, pretending to care. Which is hard with my family because my mom is always trying to get me to eat. She makes this huge Sunday dinner every week. Roast beef, mashed potatoes, the works. Her gravy is amazing, she uses the drippings and a little bit of red wine. I tried to make it once, but I burnt the flour and the whole house smelled like smoke for two days. I had to open all the windows and it was freezing outside. My neighbor, Mrs. Higgins, came over to check on me because she thought the place was on fire. She's so sweet, she always brings over these little tin boxes of cookies at Christmas. Those Danish butter cookies, you know the ones? But anyway, yeah... I don't really look forward to anything right now. It's just all flat.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a depressed mood most of the time?\n\nPatient: Didn't we kind of just talk about that? Or, wait, you asked if I was sad before. I mean... yeah, I guess it's the same thing, right? Like I was saying with that heavy blanket feeling, it's just dragging me down all day. \n\nIt's funny, the word depressed always makes me think of those old commercials on TV with the little sad bouncing blob or the wind-up toys. I always hated wind-up toys. My grandmother used to collect them and keep them on this glass shelf in her living room. She had this one that was a little monkey clashing cymbals together, and it was honestly terrifying. Its eyes were painted on all wide and staring. She had this whole collection of weird stuff, actually. Like those porcelain dolls that looked like they were watching you sleep. The traffic on my street is always so bad, especially around rush hour. People drive like absolute maniacs, completely ignoring the speed limit. The city put up one of those digital signs that flashes your speed, but I swear people just use it as a high score board. It's one of those signs with the super bright yellow LED lights. The lights are so blinding, especially when it's raining and it reflects off the wet pavement. I hate walking in the rain. My umbrella always flips inside out because the wind funnels right down my street between the apartment buildings. I had this really cute umbrella with little ducks on it that I got at a thrift store, but the wind absolutely destroyed it last month. I was so mad. The thrift store where I got it is run by this older couple, and they have the most amazing collection of vintage records in the back. But yeah... the thoughts about not waking up or stepping into traffic, they definitely scare me.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past month?\n\nPatient: Um, I haven't really weighed myself, but my appetite has just been completely all over the place. I actually had to go buy a new belt for work because my old one didn't have enough holes in it anymore. I got this really nice leather one from that same thrift store I was talking about. It has this big brass buckle that looks like a horseshoe. I don't even ride horses, but I thought it looked cool. My cousin Sarah used to ride horses when we were kids. She had this beautiful chestnut mare named Cinnamon. Cinnamon was so stubborn, she would always try to bite you if you tried to tighten the saddle too much. Sarah got thrown off once and broke her collarbone. She had to wear this really uncomfortable brace for weeks and couldn't even wash her own hair. I think I actually just mentioned that a second ago, but yeah. It's exactly that. All the things I usually love doing just feel completely flat now. Like cooking for myself, playing my guitar, going hiking, or even going over to my family’s place... I just don't care anymore. If I do go over there or try to do something, I’m really just pretending to care. 

My guitar is actually just sitting in the corner of my bedroom getting completely covered in dust right now. The dust in my apartment is out of control anyway because of all that traffic outside I was talking about. If I leave the window open even a tiny crack, this weird black soot gets all over my windowsill. I tried buying one of those tower air purifiers online, but the replacement filters are so ridiculously expensive. They want like forty dollars for a piece of folded paper. My uncle Terry told me I should just tape a cheap furnace filter to the back of a box fan. He does that in his garage where he does all his woodworking. He makes these really intricate birdhouses out of cedar wood, and his garage always smells so good, like a forest. He tried selling his birdhouses at the farmer's market last summer, but they put his booth right next to the guy selling pickled eggs, and nobody wanted to walk down that aisle because of the smell. Pickled eggs are so gross to me. The texture is just like rubber, and they look like weird alien pods in those giant glass jars. 

But um... what did you ask me? Oh, right. Yeah, I just don't have the desire to play my guitar or cook or hike or do anything I used to like. It's all just flat.

She just hides under the sofa all morning. I bought her this special calming collar, it smells like lavender, but she managed to hook her claw in it and rip it right off. She's a smart cat, part Siamese, I think. She has those bright blue eyes. But no, I wouldn't say I feel anxious or on edge, really. Just... restless, maybe.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: I don't know, not really. Like I was saying before, the stuff I usually love just... isn't doing it for me. Cooking, playing my guitar, even hiking. It's all just really flat. I feel like I'm just going through the motions, you know? Pretending to care. Like when you're at a party and you have to smile at someone's baby even though you don't really like babies. My cousin just had a baby, actually. A little girl. They named her Harper. It's a cute name, but they painted her nursery this really aggressive shade of yellow. Like a school bus. I told them it might be too bright for a baby to sleep in, but they said they wanted something cheerful. Cheerful is fine, but you need to sleep! They bought all this expensive furniture for the room too, a crib that converts into a toddler bed and then a full-size bed. It seems like a smart idea, but who knows if she'll even want that bed when she's a teenager. Teenagers are so picky. I remember when I was a teenager, I insisted on painting my room black. My mom was furious. But yeah... I just can't seem to find the joy in my usual things right now.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um, well, the really low part... the empty feeling... that's been going on for about four months now. It started way after that initial rush of energy I had when the work shifts first got all crazy. Four months is a long time, actually. It's like a whole season. Summer just kind of came and went and I barely noticed. I usually love summer, you know? The smell of sunscreen and chlorine. My friend Sarah has a pool, an above-ground one, but it's still nice. We used to sit out there on these cheap plastic lounge chairs that always left weird lines on the backs of your legs. We'd drink those hard seltzers, the black cherry ones. I don't really like the lime ones, they taste like cleaning supplies. Like Pine-Sol or something. My mom used to clean the whole house with Pine-Sol every Saturday morning. You'd wake up and your eyes would practically water from the smell of it. She was obsessed with keeping the floors shiny. But yeah, it's been about four months of just feeling really down and empty.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're empty inside, or is it more that you just can't find pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um, I think it's... it's kind of both, really. Like I said before, the stuff I usually love doing—cooking for myself, playing my guitar, going hiking, or even just going over to my family's place—it all just feels so flat right now. Like I'm just pretending to care about it all. Which is weird because my family usually has these big Sunday dinners. My aunt always makes her famous lasagna. She uses three different kinds of cheese, and she swears the secret is a tiny pinch of nutmeg in the ricotta. I tried making it once, but I accidentally used cinnamon instead of nutmeg. It was awful. My brother actually tried to eat it to be nice, but I could tell he was just choking it down. He's always been a good sport like that. We used to play basketball in the driveway for hours when we were kids until the streetlights came on. The streetlights in my neighborhood now are those new LED ones, and they're so bright they practically shine right through my bedroom window. I had to buy blackout curtains, but they're this heavy velvet material that collects so much dust. I feel like I'm constantly sneezing. But yeah, everything just feels empty and flat, like I'm just going through the motions with all the things I used to enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're sad most of the time, even if it's not always at the front of your mind?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I guess so. I mean, I'm not walking around crying all day, but it's like this heavy blanket is just sitting on me all the time. Even when I'm at work, scanning groceries. The conveyor belt just keeps moving, beep, beep, beep. We had this one lady come in last week who bought literally fifty cans of cat food. The little fancy ones with the pull tabs. She said she was feeding a colony of feral cats behind the old strip mall. I think that's nice of her, but I can't imagine how much that costs. Groceries are so expensive right now. A carton of eggs is ridiculous. I used to make omelets all the time, but now I just eat cereal. Frosted Flakes, usually. Tony the Tiger. They changed the box art recently, did you notice? He looks more... 3D now. I liked the old cartoon version better. But yeah, the sadness is just always kind of there, in the background.\n\nDoctor: Are you still interested in doing things, even if you don't feel like you enjoy them as much?\n\nPatient: Um, not really, no. Like I was saying before, the stuff I usually love doing... cooking for myself, playing my guitar, going hiking, or going over to my family's place... it all just feels so flat right now. I'm not really interested in any of it. It's like I'm just pretending to care. Which is so weird for me, especially the guitar. I have this beautiful acoustic guitar, a Taylor. I saved up for like two years to buy it. I used to keep my tips from the grocery store in this big glass jar on my dresser. It used to be a pickle jar, actually. Vlasic kosher dills. I love pickles. But you have to wash the jar out like five times with hot water and baking soda or else everything you put in it smells like garlic and vinegar. I actually tried to make my own pickles once. I bought all these pickling cucumbers from the farmer's market, and all the spices, the dill seed, the mustard seed. You have to sterilize the jars in boiling water, which made my whole apartment feel like a sauna. It was during that really bad heatwave last summer, too. The AC in my building is terrible. The maintenance guy, Frank, he's always saying he's going to fix it, but he just comes in, bangs on the vent with a wrench, and leaves. He's a nice guy though, he always gives my cat a little treat when he comes over. But yeah... I don't even bother taking the guitar out of the case anymore. I just don't have the interest.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I think so. Like I said before, it's just this heavy blanket feeling. I'm not crying all the time, but the sadness is just... sitting there. Even when I'm just watching TV or something. I was watching this documentary last night about penguins. Emperor penguins, down in Antarctica. It's crazy how they huddle together to stay warm in those blizzards, taking turns being on the outside of the circle. I hate being cold. My apartment gets so drafty in the winter because the windows are old and they don't seal right. I bought that plastic shrink wrap stuff you put over the windows with a hairdryer, but it always peels off at the corners. My cat, Barnaby, loves to chew on the plastic when it comes loose. He's a tuxedo cat, mostly black with little white paws. He knocked over a glass of water on my nightstand yesterday right onto my phone charger. I had to put it in rice. Do you know if that rice trick actually works? I read online that it's a myth and you're just supposed to leave it in front of a fan. But anyway, yeah, the sadness is just pretty constant, even if it's just hanging out in the background.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I guess I have, yeah. My appetite has been totally all over the place. Like, lately I just forget to eat, or when I do, everything just tastes like cardboard. I noticed my work pants have been fitting a lot looser, which is annoying because we have a strict uniform. Khakis and this awful polo shirt. The polo is this really bright, obnoxious red. It clashes with everything. And the fabric is so scratchy, it's like wearing a burlap sack. They make us tuck it in, too, which just looks terrible on everybody. My manager, Brenda, she's a stickler for the uniform. She actually sent a kid home last week because he was wearing black sneakers instead of brown ones. Can you believe that? Black sneakers! Like anyone is looking at the cashier's feet. He's a good kid, too, saving up for college. He wants to study marine biology. I always thought that sounded cool, swimming with dolphins and stuff. But I'm terrified of sharks. I saw Jaws when I was way too young, and it messed me up. I can't even go in the ocean past my knees. But anyway, yeah, I'm definitely losing weight because I'm just not eating much.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Um, not really. I think I mentioned it a minute ago, but yeah, no. Even the stuff I usually love... cooking for myself, playing my guitar, going hiking, or going over to my family's place... it all just feels so flat right now. Like I'm just going through the motions, pretending to care. Which is hard with my family because my mom is always trying to get me to eat. She makes this huge Sunday dinner every week. Roast beef, mashed potatoes, the works. Her gravy is amazing, she uses the drippings and a little bit of red wine. I tried to make it once, but I burnt the flour and the whole house smelled like smoke for two days. I had to open all the windows and it was freezing outside. My neighbor, Mrs. Higgins, came over to check on me because she thought the place was on fire. She's so sweet, she always brings over these little tin boxes of cookies at Christmas. Those Danish butter cookies, you know the ones? But anyway, yeah... I don't really look forward to anything right now. It's just all flat.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a depressed mood most of the time?\n\nPatient: Didn't we kind of just talk about that? Or, wait, you asked if I was sad before. I mean... yeah, I guess it's the same thing, right? Like I was saying with that heavy blanket feeling, it's just dragging me down all day. \n\nIt's funny, the word depressed always makes me think of those old commercials on TV with the little sad bouncing blob or the wind-up toys. I always hated wind-up toys. My grandmother used to collect them and keep them on this glass shelf in her living room. She had this one that was a little monkey clashing cymbals together, and it was honestly terrifying. Its eyes were painted on all wide and staring. She had this whole collection of weird stuff, actually. Like those porcelain dolls that looked like they were watching you sleep. I swear one of them moved its head once when I was staying over, but my mom said it was just the headlights from a car outside making shadows on the wall. We lived right near a really busy intersection back then, so there were always cars going by at all hours. Sometimes you'd hear people yelling out their windows at each other in the middle of the night. \n\nBut yeah... depressed, sad, whatever you want to call it. It's just pretty much there all the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of death or suicide recently?\n\nPatient: I mean... yeah, I've had some scary thoughts lately. Like, sometimes I just think maybe everyone would be better off if I just went to sleep and didn't wake up. And a couple of times, walking home from work, I actually caught myself thinking about just stepping right off the curb into traffic. The traffic on my street is always so bad, especially around rush hour. People drive like absolute maniacs, completely ignoring the speed limit. The city put up one of those digital signs that flashes your speed, but I swear people just use it as a high score board. It's one of those signs with the super bright yellow LED lights. The lights are so blinding, especially when it's raining and it reflects off the wet pavement. I hate walking in the rain. My umbrella always flips inside out because the wind funnels right down my street between the apartment buildings. I had this really cute umbrella with little ducks on it that I got at a thrift store, but the wind absolutely destroyed it last month. I was so mad. The thrift store where I got it is run by this older couple, and they have the most amazing collection of vintage records in the back. But yeah... the thoughts about not waking up or stepping into traffic, they definitely scare me.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past month?\n\nPatient: Um, I haven't really weighed myself, but my appetite has just been completely all over the place. Lately, I mostly just forget to eat entirely, or when I do, the food just tastes like cardboard. But I did notice my work pants have been fitting a lot looser recently. I actually had to go buy a new belt for work because my old one didn't have enough holes in it anymore. I got this really nice leather one from that same thrift store I was talking about. It has this big brass buckle that looks like a horseshoe. I don't even ride horses, but I thought it looked cool. My cousin Sarah used to ride horses when we were kids. She had this beautiful chestnut mare named Cinnamon. Cinnamon was so stubborn, she would always try to bite you if you tried to tighten the saddle too much. Sarah got thrown off once and broke her collarbone. She had to wear this really uncomfortable brace for weeks and couldn't even wash her own hair. Her mom had to help her in the sink, like when you go to the salon. I love going to the salon, the scalp massage they do when they wash your hair is the best part. But yeah, my pants are definitely looser.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things that still bring you a little bit of joy?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I mean, not really. Things I usually love just feel... flat now. Like, I used to love cooking for myself, playing my guitar, going hiking, or even just going over to my family's place. Now it's like I'm only pretending to care about any of it. \n\nMy brother got a new dog recently, a golden retriever puppy, and usually I'd be all over that. I love dogs. When we were growing up, we had this mutt named Buster who would follow us everywhere. He was part terrier, part who-knows-what, and he had this one ear that always stood straight up while the other one flopped over. He used to chase the mail carrier every single day, no matter how many times my dad yelled at him. Our mail carrier back then was this guy named Gary who always wore those really short blue shorts, even in the dead of winter. I never understood how his legs didn't freeze. My legs get cold so easily. I have to wear two pairs of socks to work because standing behind the register all day on that hard tile floor just freezes me right through my shoes. I bought those thick wool socks they sell for camping, but they make my work shoes feel too tight, which gave me a blister on my heel last week. Blisters are the worst, especially when they pop and get all raw. \n\nBut yeah... even seeing the new puppy, I just kind of sat there. I felt like I was supposed to be smiling, but I was just faking it. None of it really brings me joy right now.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now find no pleasure in?\n\nPatient: I think I actually just mentioned that a second ago, but yeah. It's exactly that. All the things I usually love doing just feel completely flat now. Like cooking for myself, playing my guitar, going hiking, or even going over to my family’s place... I just don't care anymore. If I do go over there or try to do something, I’m really just pretending to care. \n\nMy guitar is actually just sitting in the corner of my bedroom getting completely covered in dust right now. The dust in my apartment is out of control anyway because of all that traffic outside I was talking about. If I leave the window open even a tiny crack, this weird black soot gets all over my windowsill. I tried buying one of those tower air purifiers online, but the replacement filters are so ridiculously expensive. They want like forty dollars for a piece of folded paper. My uncle Terry told me I should just tape a cheap furnace filter to the back of a box fan. He does that in his garage where he does all his woodworking. He makes these really intricate birdhouses out of cedar wood, and his garage always smells so good, like a forest. He tried selling his birdhouses at the farmer's market last summer, but they put his booth right next to the guy selling pickled eggs, and nobody wanted to walk down that aisle because of the smell. Pickled eggs are so gross to me. The texture is just like rubber, and they look like weird alien pods in those giant glass jars. \n\nBut um... what did you ask me? Oh, right. Yeah, I just don't have the desire to play my guitar or cook or hike or do anything I used to like. It's all just flat.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I mean... yeah, I guess it's the same thing, right? Like I was saying with that heavy blanket feeling, it's just dragging me down all day. \n\nIt's funny, the word depressed always makes me think of those old commercials on TV with the little sad bouncing blob or the wind-up toys. I always hated wind-up toys. My grandmother used to collect them and keep them on this glass shelf in her living room. She had this one that was a little monkey clashing cymbals together, and it was honestly terrifying. Its eyes were painted on all wide and staring. She had this whole collection of weird stuff, actually. Like those porcelain dolls that looked like they were watching you sleep. I swear one of them moved its head once when I was staying over, but my mom said it was just the headlights from a car outside making shadows on the wall. We lived right near a really busy intersection back then, so there were always cars going by at all hours. Sometimes you'd hear people yelling out their windows at each other in the middle of the night. \n\nBut yeah... depressed, sad, whatever you want to call it. It's just pretty much there all the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of death or suicide recently?\n\nPatient: I mean... yeah, I've had some scary thoughts lately. Like, sometimes I just think maybe everyone would be better off if I just went to sleep and didn't wake up. The traffic on my street is always so bad, especially around rush hour. People drive like absolute maniacs, completely ignoring the speed limit. The city put up one of those digital signs that flashes your speed, but I swear people just use it as a high score board. It's one of those signs with the super bright yellow LED lights. The lights are so blinding, especially when it's raining and it reflects off the wet pavement. I hate walking in the rain. My umbrella always flips inside out because the wind funnels right down my street between the apartment buildings. I had this really cute umbrella with little ducks on it that I got at a thrift store, but the wind absolutely destroyed it last month. I was so mad. I guess so? I don't know if I'd call it depressed, but it's just this heavy feeling that won't go away. It feels like I'm moving through molasses. Molasses is such a weird word, isn't it? My grandma used to make these molasses cookies every Christmas. They were so soft and chewy, and she always kept them in this old metal tin that had a picture of a snowy village on it. The tin was actually from some fruitcake she got in the eighties, but she reused it every single year. She hated fruitcake. She always said it was just a brick of candied regret. I think fruitcake is mostly just those weird little neon green and red jelly things. What even are those? They don't taste like any fruit I've ever had. I bought a fruit salad from the deli near my work once, and the honeydew melon was so hard it practically crunched. I hate when they fill the whole container with just honeydew and cantaloupe to save money. The grapes are always the best part, but there's never enough of them. I usually end up picking out the grapes and throwing the rest away, which I know is a waste of money. Money has been so tight lately anyway with my hours getting cut at the store. 

But um, yeah. I guess I do feel depressed. Just really heavy and tired all the time.

She uses this weird app to send out the shifts and it never updates right on my phone. My phone is so old though, the screen is cracked right across the front camera so all my selfies look like I'm underwater. I dropped it in the parking lot at the grocery store. The pavement there is terrible, full of potholes. But yeah, now even when I’m completely exhausted, I wake up super early and my mind is just already going, like a million miles an hour.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or on edge?\n\nPatient: Anxious? Um... not really. I don't feel like, nervous or anything. Just... like I said, my mind is just going so fast. It's not a worried fast, just... fast. Like when you're on the highway and everyone is zooming by. The highway by my apartment is getting totally repaved right now. They start construction at like, 5 AM, with those huge floodlights and all the beeping trucks backing up. Beep, beep, beep. It drives my cat absolutely crazy. She just hides under the sofa all morning. I bought her this special calming collar, it smells like lavender, but she managed to hook her claw in it and rip it right off. She's a smart cat, part Siamese, I think. She has those bright blue eyes. But no, I wouldn't say I feel anxious or on edge, really. Just... restless, maybe.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: I don't know, not really. Like I was saying before, the stuff I usually love just... isn't doing it for me. Cooking, playing my guitar, even hiking. It's all just really flat. I feel like I'm just going through the motions, you know? Pretending to care. Like when you're at a party and you have to smile at someone's baby even though you don't really like babies. My cousin just had a baby, actually. A little girl. They named her Harper. It's a cute name, but they painted her nursery this really aggressive shade of yellow. Like a school bus. I told them it might be too bright for a baby to sleep in, but they said they wanted something cheerful. Cheerful is fine, but you need to sleep! They bought all this expensive furniture for the room too, a crib that converts into a toddler bed and then a full-size bed. It seems like a smart idea, but who knows if she'll even want that bed when she's a teenager. Teenagers are so picky. I remember when I was a teenager, I insisted on painting my room black. My mom was furious. But yeah... I just can't seem to find the joy in my usual things right now.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um, well, the really low part... the empty feeling... that's been going on for about four months now. It started way after that initial rush of energy I had when the work shifts first got all crazy. Four months is a long time, actually. It's like a whole season. Summer just kind of came and went and I barely noticed. I usually love summer, you know? The smell of sunscreen and chlorine. My friend Sarah has a pool, an above-ground one, but it's still nice. We used to sit out there on these cheap plastic lounge chairs that always left weird lines on the backs of your legs. We'd drink those hard seltzers, the black cherry ones. I don't really like the lime ones, they taste like cleaning supplies. Like Pine-Sol or something. My mom used to clean the whole house with Pine-Sol every Saturday morning. You'd wake up and your eyes would practically water from the smell of it. She was obsessed with keeping the floors shiny. But yeah, it's been about four months of just feeling really down and empty.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're empty inside, or is it more that you just can't find pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um, I think it's... it's kind of both, really. Like I said before, the stuff I usually love doing—cooking for myself, playing my guitar, going hiking, or even just going over to my family's place—it all just feels so flat right now. Like I'm just pretending to care about it all. Which is weird because my family usually has these big Sunday dinners. My aunt always makes her famous lasagna. She uses three different kinds of cheese, and she swears the secret is a tiny pinch of nutmeg in the ricotta. I tried making it once, but I accidentally used cinnamon instead of nutmeg. It was awful. My brother actually tried to eat it to be nice, but I could tell he was just choking it down. He's always been a good sport like that. We used to play basketball in the driveway for hours when we were kids until the streetlights came on. The streetlights in my neighborhood now are those new LED ones, and they're so bright they practically shine right through my bedroom window. I'm not really interested in any of it. It's like I'm just pretending to care. Which is so weird for me, especially the guitar. I have this beautiful acoustic guitar, a Taylor. But you have to wash the jar out like five times with hot water and baking soda or else everything you put in it smells like garlic and vinegar. I actually tried to make my own pickles once. I bought all these pickling cucumbers from the farmer's market, and all the spices, the dill seed, the mustard seed. You have to sterilize the jars in boiling water, which made my whole apartment feel like a sauna. It was during that really bad heatwave last summer, too. The AC in my building is terrible. He's a nice guy though, he always gives my cat a little treat when he comes over. But yeah... I don't even bother taking the guitar out of the case anymore. I just don't have the interest.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I think so. Like I said before, it's just this heavy blanket feeling. Emperor penguins, down in Antarctica. It's crazy how they huddle together to stay warm in those blizzards, taking turns being on the outside of the circle. I hate being cold. My apartment gets so drafty in the winter because the windows are old and they don't seal right. I bought that plastic shrink wrap stuff you put over the windows with a hairdryer, but it always peels off at the corners. My cat, Barnaby, loves to chew on the plastic when it comes loose. He's a tuxedo cat, mostly black with little white paws. He knocked over a glass of water on my nightstand yesterday right onto my phone charger. I had to put it in rice. It clashes with everything. And the fabric is so scratchy, it's like wearing a burlap sack. They make us tuck it in, too, which just looks terrible on everybody. You have to sterilize the jars in boiling water, which made my whole apartment feel like a sauna. It was during that really bad heatwave last summer, too. The AC in my building is terrible. The maintenance guy, Frank, he's always saying he's going to fix it, but he just comes in, bangs on the vent with a wrench, and leaves. He's a nice guy though, he always gives my cat a little treat when he comes over. But yeah... I don't even bother taking the guitar out of the case anymore. I just don't have the interest.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: I mean, yeah, I think so. Like I said before, it's just this heavy blanket feeling. I'm not crying all the time, but the sadness is just... sitting there. Even when I'm just watching TV or something. I was watching this documentary last night about penguins. Emperor penguins, down in Antarctica. It's crazy how they huddle together to stay warm in those blizzards, taking turns being on the outside of the circle. I hate being cold. My apartment gets so drafty in the winter because the windows are old and they don't seal right. I bought that plastic shrink wrap stuff you put over the windows with a hairdryer, but it always peels off at the corners. My cat, Barnaby, loves to chew on the plastic when it comes loose. He's a tuxedo cat, mostly black with little white paws. He knocked over a glass of water on my nightstand yesterday right onto my phone charger. I had to put it in rice. Do you know if that rice trick actually works? I read online that it's a myth and you're just supposed to leave it in front of a fan. But anyway, yeah, the sadness is just pretty constant, even if it's just hanging out in the background.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: I guess I have, yeah. My appetite has been totally all over the place. Like, lately I just forget to eat, or when I do, everything just tastes like cardboard. I noticed my work pants have been fitting a lot looser, which is annoying because we have a strict uniform. Khakis and this awful polo shirt. The polo is this really bright, obnoxious red. It clashes with everything. And the fabric is so scratchy, it's like wearing a burlap sack. They make us tuck it in, too, which just looks terrible on everybody. My manager, Brenda, she's a stickler for the uniform. She actually sent a kid home last week because he was wearing black sneakers instead of brown ones. Can you believe that? Black sneakers! Like anyone is looking at the cashier's feet. He's a good kid, too, saving up for college. He wants to study marine biology. I always thought that sounded cool, swimming with dolphins and stuff. But I'm terrified of sharks. I saw Jaws when I was way too young, and it messed me up. I can't even go in the ocean past my knees. But anyway, yeah, I'm definitely losing weight because I'm just not eating much.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Um, not really. I think I mentioned it a minute ago, but yeah, no. Even the stuff I usually love... cooking for myself, playing my guitar, going hiking, or going over to my family's place... it all just feels so flat right now. Like I'm just going through the motions, pretending to care. Which is hard with my family because my mom is always trying to get me to eat. She makes this huge Sunday dinner every week. Roast beef, mashed potatoes, the works. Her gravy is amazing, she uses the drippings and a little bit of red wine. I tried to make it once, but I burnt the flour and the whole house smelled like smoke for two days. I had to open all the windows and it was freezing outside. My neighbor, Mrs. Higgins, came over to check on me because she thought the place was on fire. She's so sweet, she always brings over these little tin boxes of cookies at Christmas. Those Danish butter cookies, you know the ones? But anyway, yeah... I don't really look forward to anything right now. It's just all flat.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a depressed mood most of the time?\n\nPatient: Didn't we kind of just talk about that? Or, wait, you asked if I was sad before. I mean... yeah, I guess it's the same thing, right? Like I was saying with that heavy blanket feeling, it's just dragging me down all day. \n\nIt's funny, the word depressed always makes me think of those old commercials on TV with the little sad bouncing blob or the wind-up toys. I always hated wind-up toys. My grandmother used to collect them and keep them on this glass shelf in her living room. She had this one that was a little monkey clashing cymbals together, and it was honestly terrifying. Its eyes were painted on all wide and staring. She had this whole collection of weird stuff, actually. Like those porcelain dolls that looked like they were watching you sleep. I swear one of them moved its head once when I was staying over, but my mom said it was just the headlights from a car outside making shadows on the wall. We lived right near a really busy intersection back then, so there were always cars going by at all hours. Sometimes you'd hear people yelling out their windows at each other in the middle of the night. \n\nBut yeah... depressed, sad, whatever you want to call it. It's just pretty much there all the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of death or suicide recently?\n\nPatient: I mean... yeah, I've had some scary thoughts lately. Like, sometimes I just think maybe everyone would be better off if I just went to sleep and didn't wake up. And a couple of times, walking home from work, I actually caught myself thinking about just stepping right off the curb into traffic. The traffic on my street is always so bad, especially around rush hour. People drive like absolute maniacs, completely ignoring the speed limit. The city put up one of those digital signs that flashes your speed, but I swear people just use it as a high score board. It's one of those signs with the super bright yellow LED lights. The lights are so blinding, especially when it's raining and it reflects off the wet pavement. I hate walking in the rain. My umbrella always flips inside out because the wind funnels right down my street between the apartment buildings. I had this really cute umbrella with little ducks on it that I got at a thrift store, but the wind absolutely destroyed it last month. I was so mad. The thrift store where I got it is run by this older couple, and they have the most amazing collection of vintage records in the back. But yeah... the thoughts about not waking up or stepping into traffic, they definitely scare me.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past month?\n\nPatient: Um, I haven't really weighed myself, but my appetite has just been completely all over the place. Lately, I mostly just forget to eat entirely, or when I do, the food just tastes like cardboard. But I did notice my work pants have been fitting a lot looser recently. I actually had to go buy a new belt for work because my old one didn't have enough holes in it anymore. I got this really nice leather one from that same thrift store I was talking about. It has this big brass buckle that looks like a horseshoe. I don't even ride horses, but I thought it looked cool. My cousin Sarah used to ride horses when we were kids. She had this beautiful chestnut mare named Cinnamon. Cinnamon was so stubborn, she would always try to bite you if you tried to tighten the saddle too much. Sarah got thrown off once and broke her collarbone. She had to wear this really uncomfortable brace for weeks and couldn't even wash her own hair. Her mom had to help her in the sink, like when you go to the salon. I love going to the salon, the scalp massage they do when they wash your hair is the best part. But yeah, my pants are definitely looser.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things that still bring you a little bit of joy?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I mean, not really. Things I usually love just feel... flat now. Like, I used to love cooking for myself, playing my guitar, going hiking, or even just going over to my family's place. Now it's like I'm only pretending to care about any of it. \n\nMy brother got a new dog recently, a golden retriever puppy, and usually I'd be all over that. I love dogs. When we were growing up, we had this mutt named Buster who would follow us everywhere. He was part terrier, part who-knows-what, and he had this one ear that always stood straight up while the other one flopped over. He used to chase the mail carrier every single day, no matter how many times my dad yelled at him. Our mail carrier back then was this guy named Gary who always wore those really short blue shorts, even in the dead of winter. I never understood how his legs didn't freeze. My legs get cold so easily. I have to wear two pairs of socks to work because standing behind the register all day on that hard tile floor just freezes me right through my shoes. I bought those thick wool socks they sell for camping, but they make my work shoes feel too tight, which gave me a blister on my heel last week. Blisters are the worst, especially when they pop and get all raw. \n\nBut yeah... even seeing the new puppy, I just kind of sat there. I felt like I was supposed to be smiling, but I was just faking it. None of it really brings me joy right now.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now find no pleasure in?\n\nPatient: I think I actually just mentioned that a second ago, but yeah. It's exactly that. All the things I usually love doing just feel completely flat now. Like cooking for myself, playing my guitar, going hiking, or even going over to my family’s place... I just don't care anymore. If I do go over there or try to do something, I’m really just pretending to care. \n\nMy guitar is actually just sitting in the corner of my bedroom getting completely covered in dust right now. The dust in my apartment is out of control anyway because of all that traffic outside I was talking about. If I leave the window open even a tiny crack, this weird black soot gets all over my windowsill. I tried buying one of those tower air purifiers online, but the replacement filters are so ridiculously expensive. They want like forty dollars for a piece of folded paper. My uncle Terry told me I should just tape a cheap furnace filter to the back of a box fan. He does that in his garage where he does all his woodworking. He makes these really intricate birdhouses out of cedar wood, and his garage always smells so good, like a forest. He tried selling his birdhouses at the farmer's market last summer, but they put his booth right next to the guy selling pickled eggs, and nobody wanted to walk down that aisle because of the smell. Pickled eggs are so gross to me. The texture is just like rubber, and they look like weird alien pods in those giant glass jars. \n\nBut um... what did you ask me? Oh, right. Yeah, I just don't have the desire to play my guitar or cook or hike or do anything I used to like. It's all just flat.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a depressed mood right now?\n\nPatient: I guess so? I don't know if I'd call it depressed, but it's just this heavy feeling that won't go away. It feels like I'm moving through molasses. Molasses is such a weird word, isn't it? My grandma used to make these molasses cookies every Christmas. They were so soft and chewy, and she always kept them in this old metal tin that had a picture of a snowy village on it. The tin was actually from some fruitcake she got in the eighties, but she reused it every single year. She hated fruitcake. She always said it was just a brick of candied regret. I think fruitcake is mostly just those weird little neon green and red jelly things. What even are those? They don't taste like any fruit I've ever had. I bought a fruit salad from the deli near my work once, and the honeydew melon was so hard it practically crunched. I hate when they fill the whole container with just honeydew and cantaloupe to save money. The grapes are always the best part, but there's never enough of them. I usually end up picking out the grapes and throwing the rest away, which I know is a waste of money. Money has been so tight lately anyway with my hours getting cut at the store. \n\nBut um, yeah. I guess I do feel depressed. Just really heavy and tired all the time."
